# HIP Docs Index — single source of truth
Read this FIRST every session. Lists every subject, its category, current file, and status.
STATUS values: PLAN | IN_PROGRESS | BUILT | SUPERSEDED | STALE
Last updated: 2026-08-14 (Mountain Time, Voice 42)

## Naming law
docs/<category>/<SUBJECT>__<slug>__v<YYYYMMDD_HHMM>.md  (Mountain Time)
Never overwrite. New thought = new file. Update the LATEST_<subject> symlink and this index.

## Categories
specs | planning | requirements | dispatches | reviews | testing | research-technical | research-market | transfers | epistemic | debt | general

## DISPATCH LEDGER (HIP Advisor series)

**One row per dispatch, findable by ID alone.** Added 2026-08-07 by Bill's naming-law
amendment (`CLAUDE.md`, "Dispatch docs lead with their ID"). From **HA-14 forward** the
filename itself starts with the ID; **HA-01 through HA-13 are backfilled here instead of
being renamed**, because renaming would break every path already cited in this file, in
MANIFEST, in prior dispatch docs, in commit messages and in `LATEST_*` symlinks.

**The ledger is not retired by the naming change.** It also carries the dispatches that
produced NO doc — a number with no row is exactly the gap this table exists to close.

Backfilled from the docs themselves (each doc's own `**HA-nn**` line and H1 title), not
from recollection. Predecessor series `D-R-nnn` ended at `D-R-196` and is not listed here.

Fernet authenticates its ciphertext but **carries no context**, so a valid encrypted utterance could be moved between sessions, turns or members and still verify. Every envelope now binds **`{v, session_id, turn_id, member_id, content_type, epoch}`** as AAD, canonicalized by the ledger's own `_canon`, **re-derived from the envelope's own fields on decrypt** plus an explicit session-equality check — **two independent gates**, and a mismatch raises before any plaintext exists. **(2) THE KEY IS SPLIT, and this is what makes the operator answer true.** A wrap is sealed to a member's LONG-LIVED X25519 key, so wrapping the content key directly would mean operator access **never dies**. Instead `content_key = HKDF-SHA256(share, salt=session_salt, info=b"hip-session-content:v1:"+session_id)` — the **share** is wrapped, the **salt is never wrapped, never written, and dies at `end()`.** **ITEM 4 ANSWERED WITH BOTH HALVES SHOWN: the persisted wrap STILL UNWRAPS (the honest half — `open_persisted_wrap` exists to demonstrate it) and the READ STILL FAILS**, because the share alone cannot reconstruct the key. **(3) COMMITMENT DOMAIN-SEPARATED:** exactly `{"v":"HIP-TURN-COMMITMENT-v1", session_id, turn_id, member_id, text}` canonicalized by `_canon` — one convention, not two — so **two record types cannot produce interchangeable commitments**, asserted by a test that moves the separator. The commitment is now bound to session and turn as well as member. **(4) LIFECYCLE ANSWERED IN CODE VIA AUTHORIZATION EPOCHS. THE EXPLICIT QUESTION: a member who joins mid-session CANNOT read earlier turns** — authorising opens a new epoch and they get no wrap for earlier ones; **least privilege, because they were not present, and the one-key-per-session alternative silently hands a late joiner the entire backlog.** Revocation is **forward-only** (they already saw those turns; retroactive removal would be theatre); an operator change is a revoke plus an authorise; **a reconnecting member is NOT re-authorised and a test asserts the epoch does not advance**; `end()` idempotent; **crash-before-`end()` loses everything because nothing was ever written** — the registry has no persistence layer deliberately; revoking the last member is refused. **(5) FIVE DEFECT CLASSES PROVEN RED THEN RESTORED:** AAD removed → 5 red; salt removed → 1 red; epochs removed → 1 red; domain separation removed → 2 red; **memory overclaim introduced → 1 red.** Zero `DEFECT` markers remain, 30/30 green. Unrecognised is AMBIGUOUS and **not DECLINED**: decline is terminal and R8 makes spending permanent, so the offer stays open rather than being spent on a guess. **Raw prose stops at the boundary** — Classification carries the matched entry and a hash, never the words, and `apply_response` has NO text parameter, asserted by signature. `resolves` and `grants_authority` split after a first draft exposed one flag that **made a DECLINE read as granting authority**. ResponseKind gains QUESTION and INVALID, both NON-RESOLVING, with a whole-enum test pinning that exactly two kinds resolve. Regression kept as a test: the first cut classified "do it" and "do not" as QUESTIONS. 76 tests. Vocabulary breadth filed for Bill — a ratification question, not a mechanism one. **SEG 3 — A16 narrowing and revocation** (`e18a0ae`): **revocation is NOT an offer transition** — R19 has no offer and no trigger, so R23's closed five stay closed and it appends its own `authority_change` event kind. Derivative consequences enforced. **Fault twins both directions** including the BOUNDED one — a revocation that took too much would pass every access-denied assertion while destroying the product. **No parameter exists by which revocation could be delayed**, asserted by signature: an option to delay IS the mechanism R19 forbids. 23 tests. **TD-R-184 FILED, NOT FIXED — item 12 applied rather than quoted:** `authority_manifest_for` folds `update(after)` then `difference_update(before)`, right for one event and wrong for a sequence, so **a member who accepts two offers has the first vanish from R25's cumulative manifest**; hidden because every existing caller passes an empty scope_before. | **REQ_GOVERNED_REALTIME_INTERACTION — HIP owns governance; the realtime model owns INTERACTION QUALITY ONLY** | `requirements/REQ_GOVERNED_REALTIME_INTERACTION__hip-owns-governance-the-realtime-model-owns-interaction-quality-only__v20260815_1624.md` | **PLAN — docs only; the build follows review** | NC 36, 2026-08-15. Bill's ruling B verbatim as the spine: **HIP owns authenticated identity, subject resolution, authorization, admitted context, disclosure, consent and writes; the external/realtime model owns prosody, timing and conversational flow ONLY, operates only on context HIP already admitted, and does NOT become a second governance authority.** **THE LAST CLAUSE IS A NEGATIVE, so the acceptance is structural rather than behavioural** — a negative is not satisfied by a model that behaves well but by a boundary it cannot cross when it misbehaves. **Grounded at each tree's own HEAD:** the one decision point both modalities cross (`kernel.py:353`, called from `governed_turn` at `:197`, nc-b0 `62c0848`) is the admission boundary and this REQ adds no second one — *if a realtime design needs a decision the kernel does not make, that is a kernel change, never a decision the channel makes for itself*; NC 34's barriers bind (`evidence_record.py:83`/`:106`, main `940ab5d`) because **handing context to a realtime channel IS reaching a model**; `/ws/voice` stays flag-off, referenced and NOT relitigated (NC 19's *"REMOVAL FROM THE ACTIVE ATTACK SURFACE — never describe it as a realtime migration"*), and **this REQ is the thing NC 19 was waiting for, not a request to revisit it**. **TD-V-040 IS NAMED AS THE STANDING COUNTEREXAMPLE** — a third model-reaching, reply-delivering path with NEITHER barrier — and read as the failure MODE: *the gap was not a wrong decision, it was a path nobody enumerated*, which is exactly what a realtime integration produces more of. **The banked dual-model spec v2 is RECONCILED, not superseded**: §5's *ConversationObservation grants no authority* and §6's *Option 3 REJECTED* are adopted and GENERALISED to any provider, while §7's Moshi-specific M0-M5 staging is left standing. **One honest discrepancy recorded:** the A1 plan notes `AuthorizedResponseEnvelope`/`ConversationObservation` **do not exist in code**, so the REQ specifies a PROPERTY rather than a class — naming a type that does not exist would make the acceptance unwritable. **Acceptance sketch:** the channel can never emit content derived from unadmitted context (with an ADVERSARIAL half — unreachable even when prompted directly — and an ANTI-VACUITY half, because a channel that emits nothing passes and is useless); a governance refusal terminates or redirects deterministically, **including the hard mid-stream case, named and left open**; and the epistemic record distinguishes realtime-delivered from HIP-delivered content, without which the first clause is unauditable after the fact. **THREE OPEN DECISIONS STAGED, NOT TAKEN:** which provider(s); where the audio boundary sits (STT/TTS local vs remote — *a privacy-boundary decision, not a latency decision*); and mid-stream refusal behaviour, which this REQ DISCOVERED rather than inherited and staged rather than defaulted. Lane-B: drafted in a temp worktree off `roadmap`, pushed as `nc36-tmp:roadmap`, removed after — no lock on any code tree. |
| REQ_UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE_DETECTOR **AMENDMENT 4** (NC 31): third-person medical frames, **the SUBJECT BOUNDARY**, and Amendment 3's Q1 RULED | `requirements/REQ_UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE_DETECTOR__b1-structural-stop-on-missing-conversational-state__v20260814_2056.md` | **PLAN — amendment filed before the first line of NC 31 code** | NC 31, 2026-08-15. **CLOSES NC26-F2 AND ANSWERS AMENDMENT 3's Q1** — both filed by sessions that declined to decide them; this is the second run of that mechanism in the sequence (NC 25 filed NC25-F1, NC 26 built it; NC 26 filed NC26-F2 and Q1, NC 31 builds them). **Q1 IS RULED CLARIFY-AS-BUILT: Amendment 3's tier STANDS UNCHANGED**, and Bill's addition sharpens it — *"NEVER a pending write from uncertainty alone"*, which is a claim about the PARK MACHINERY and not about the class label, so **a twin asserting `cls is AMBIGUOUS_WRITE` does not discharge it**: the twin must read 0 parked rows from `harness.confirmation_gate` and 0 model calls from `harness.model_calls` (P7.2). **RULING 2 IS THE HEART: medical-frame detection establishes SENSITIVITY/WRITE SEMANTICS ONLY and NOTHING about who the fact is about.** Proven four ways — no first-person write class for a third-party frame (P7.8); **STRUCTURALLY — `MedicalSplit` carries exactly `cls` and `reason` and neither is a subject, so the shortcut is impossible rather than merely untaken** (P7.9); the resolution step still runs downstream at `voice_orch.py:2844` (P7.10); and an unresolvable subject refuses with the medical frame buying no bypass (P7.11). **A DEFECT MEASURED AT CLAIM TIME AND FIXED HERE: `"my mother takes metformin 500mg"` classifies FACT_ASSERTION_WRITE with reason literally `first-person+assert-verb:takes`** — `_FIRST_PERSON` matches the POSSESSIVE `my`, so a turn about the speaker's mother is read as a first-person write assertion; NAME subjects route correctly, so the gap is the possessive-kinship subject specifically. **It pre-dates NC 25 and NC 26 (it is NC 15's `_FIRST_PERSON` including my/me) and is the ruling-2 shortcut arriving through the SUBJECT TEST rather than through the frames.** The fix is subject-directed, not verb-directed, so it covers `takes` and every other verb (P7.12), and **the severity is bounded honestly (P7.13): what is measured is a wrong CLASSIFICATION asserting first-person subject semantics — whether a wrong-subject write actually lands is downstream and NOT measured here.** Reconciled-Against `0ee55fb`. |

Bill made it. **A session declined to widen a boundary on its own authority, said so in writing, and the ruling followed — the mechanism working, not a delay.** **THREE frames are added, not four: `"I'm taking X"` ALREADY lands in governance at `bfb09df`** via NC 25's take-verb, so it is TWINNED rather than reimplemented and there is no second copy to drift. **THE ONE INTERPRETIVE CALL IS FLAGGED FOR OVERRULE (Q1):** constraint 2 says an unknown-but-plausible object takes *"the RESTRICTIVE path (medical governance / clarification)"* and to *"fail toward asking"* — both (a) and (b) are governance, and NC 26 reads it as a TWO-TIER rule: take/taking → (a) always (unchanged); on/started/stopped → (a) when corroborated by a dosage, brand-shaped token or lexicon hit, else **(b) AMBIGUOUS_WRITE — clarification, NO model, NO write**, which is the more restrictive of the two because it writes NOTHING. A NINE-STEP build order is now the contract's own sequence, step 2 (session key/custody) named as the precondition for step 5 (the read path). Statuses unchanged; the §7 acceptance still fails today.** Originally drafted at HA-47, 2026-08-12, on Bill's ruling that row 19 needs a storage contract before any build, with his constraint **"the dashboard never decrypts arbitrary transcript files"** treated as binding — it eliminates one otherwise-obvious option at Q4. **ESTABLISHED READ-ONLY: of SIX consumers of `logs/transcript/`, exactly ONE needs the words** — the dashboard's live band via `TurnBubble`, which renders only `speaker`, `member_id`, `tier`, `text` — **and it only ever shows the CURRENT session.** The consent vignette's 40-character prefix is an **identity match, not a content assertion** (it finds *which* record is turn 2; the assertion is `tier=='escalate'`) **and it is one branch of an OR whose other branch already correlates without plaintext**, so dropping it loses no coverage. Third consumer found (`integration_harness` DEMO-002) needs `member_id`/`speaker` only. **SIX PROPOSED DISPOSITIONS: (Q1)** words recoverable only while the session is live; durable records keep metadata + keyed commitment. **(Q2)** recoverable for the speaking member and the live operator, nobody else and not afterwards. **(Q3)** per-session content key wrapped to each member's key, held in memory only; per-turn would hit TD-R-172's key explosion, per-member is coarser than Q6 needs; **this is Q2/step-4 custody work and must be sequenced with it.** **(Q4)** `/api/transcript` **stops reading transcript files entirely** — the band is fed from an in-memory session buffer, making Bill's constraint structural rather than a rule. **(Q5)** correlate on `turn_id` with a keyed-commitment fallback, and `query_hash` should BECOME a keyed commitment. **(Q6)** member erasure destroys the member's key material while **shared/household keys are never destroyed — remove the wrap, rotate to a new epoch**; records are not deleted, a field goes opaque, the HA-46A shape. **TD-R-189's ORDERING RULE IS A BINDING CLAUSE:** commitments are minted while the subject's key exists; plaintext is never removed after the key that could commit to it is gone — mint, verify, then erase, with any key-less turn reported as metadata-only and never repaired by minting a key after the fact. **This makes the ratified build order's safe sequencing a REQUIREMENT rather than a coincidence.** **THE WINDOW IS OPEN NOW: bill/maya/sam all still hold keys, so 100% of the 27,732 turns are committable today** — the opposite of HA-46A's zero — **and it closes when Q2's key destruction runs.** **MIGRATION (PROPOSED, gated on ratification):** the 425 `.jsonl` files convert (drop `text`, add `text_commitment` + `turn_id`, entry count preserved, atomic/dry-run/idempotent); **the 425 `.txt` files are ERASED, not converted** — their entire body IS the words, so a stripped `.txt` is an empty frame with no reader, **permanently removing the only human-readable rendering of every recorded conversation**; the demo sees everything live and **nothing historical**, a genuine capability loss named so it is ratified with eyes open. Surfaces 20/21 are explicitly NOT in this migration. Symlink LATEST_REQ_TRANSCRIPT_STORAGE.md points here |

| Confidence discipline (truth track): perception vs policy — now carries Stage 5 in full | requirements/REQ_CONFIDENCE_DISCIPLINE__truth-track__v20260721_0945.md | NOT MET | docs-only architecture REQ, ratified by Bill: confidence governs readiness to decide (abstain/clarify/hedge), policy governs permission, confidence never creates permission; no unified confidence scalar -- per-stage typed state plus raw score in one turn RECORD; propagation is dominance (most conservative upstream state wins, a fluent downstream guess is not new evidence); fallback keyed to WHICH stage is uncertain (who->withhold+step-up, what-asked->clarify, whether-true->hedge for an authorized reader -- identity/subject uncertainty blocks access, fact-truth uncertainty is presentation-only); voice section ratifies device-key-is-identity/voiceprint-is-hint (matches built voice_orch.py:1405-1471), reads may hedge but writes require readback-confirm, slot-level confidence on critical tokens, case-independent subject resolution needed for voice (text heuristic is capitalization-based, not portable); | Crypto Stage 4 Phase 4: recovery and custody exit | requirements/REQ_CRYPTO_P4_RECOVERY_EVICTION__stage4-phase4-amended-hip36-mt2gate__v20260721_1511.md | MET | BUILT 2026-07-21 (Bill-approved acceptance test, "widest blast radius on the read side"): the core defect — dyad_registry.mark_exited() was classification-only, a revoked custodian's key kept decrypting — is closed by a real atomic re-key. New harness/quorum.py: genuine 2-of-3 Shamir secret sharing over GF(2^521-1), a single share information-theoretically useless (Shamir's own proof, not a policy check) — this is what makes RE1 a cryptographic fact, not an app-level count. harness/dyad_registry.py gains atomic_rekey_dyad(): mints a fresh dyad keypair, re-wraps every affected fact's DEK (ciphertext untouched, only the wrap moves), re-seals to remaining custodians, destroys every old wrap including the exited custodian's, re-escrows the new key under quorum for next time; old D_priv comes from a remaining custodian's local unwrap or, evicting a sole custodian, quorum reconstruction. New harness/custody_exit.py orchestrates REQ_PARTITION_CUSTODY #6's three revocation tiers (recipient-unilateral / authority-initiated need no quorum; peer-initiated always quorum-gated — the deliberate asymmetry against a peer evicting a peer), the continuity rule (RE6, flagged HEL event if an exit leaves zero custodians), and the contested-PoA freeze (RE7, a disputed share is refused outright); also removes an exited custodian from the same recipient's care team if held. provision_member_recovery()/recover_member_key() build RE2 (crypto design s4.2's recovery-blob construction — a lost device's key reconstructed via quorum, restored to the local keystore). Wired into eval/harnesslib/layer7_crypto_v2.py: MT2-DECRYPT-REVOKE turned from its NAMED-PENDING skip() into a real PASS; new RE1-RE7 scenarios each with a named fault-injection (lone-share rejection, operator-alone recovery block, sole-custodian quorum eviction, HEL completeness, sibling-dyad non-interference, silent-continuity rejection, disputed-instrument rejection). Verified: == L7: 23/23 (PS1-PS4, DK1-4, N1/N2/N4/P1/P4, 13-row table rows 1-9 + 10-13 all green, no regression); == L7V2: 21/22 (1 pre-existing opt-in skip, unrelated), MT2-DECRYPT-REVOKE + RE1-RE7 all PASS; RATCHET PASS. eval/injection_harness.py: 9/11, identical pre-existing baseline (two unrelated failures), confirmed not a regression. | D-103 dispatch: A12 re-tiered LIVE with its check written in the same edit; **R12 MET** | requirements/REQ_STRUCTURAL_CEILING__dimensioned-collection-limit__v20260731_2129.md (section 16, R12) + eval/test_ceiling_audience_a12.py | MET (R12) | D-103, 2026-08-02. **Bill's rulings: A12 CONTRADICTED-XFAIL -> LIVE, and R12 MET.** Nothing else ruled. Reconciled-Against f074f6a. **A12 HAD NO FIXTURE AND THAT WAS CORRECT, NOT AN OVERSIGHT:** CONTRADICTED-XFAIL meant it failed against a RATIFIED design rather than an unbuilt feature, so the sub-tier said it needed a RULING, not a test. Ruling came D-92 ('fix the code, not the rule'), fix D-102 (f074f6a), **check and tier together at D-103 — splitting them reds the suite** (a CONTRADICTED row whose check passes is an XPASS; a LIVE row with no check asserts nothing), same pattern as A1 at D-100. **CHECK PASSED ON FIRST RUN — the step-4 STOP did not fire, so the ruling rested on a correct premise.** eval/test_ceiling_audience_a12.py, **14 cases**, registered in run_harness.sh, BEHAVIOURAL against apply_injection_contract (a source scan would pass on a cap that exists and never fires, and whether the cap FIRES is the whole subject of R12). Built from D-102's four executed cases, all four reproduced exactly. **AGGREGATION:** 10 facts an author owns about another subject -> **8 admitted / 2 denied deny_aggregation_cap** (owner_permit_cap default 8). Asserted **PER SUBJECT, NOT PER TURN** — two subjects x 10 facts yields 8 each, not 8 total, because R12 bounds the file built on ONE person and a per-turn cap would be a different requirement wearing the same number. Capped facts are removed from injected_fact_ids, so the record cannot attest an injection that never reached the prompt. Under-cap traffic untouched (a cap that fires below threshold is a throttle, not a cap). **SELF-EXEMPT, THE LOAD-BEARING HALF:** 10 facts a member owns about THEMSELVES -> **10 admitted, 0 capped**, still untouched at 3x the cap. This carries R12's FIRST clause — **a cap that also throttled a member's own record would satisfy 'cannot build a file on you' by BREAKING 'can read back what they said', and would be invisible in any aggregate count**, so it is asserted on its own, twice. **DERIVATIVES:** a derived fact ABOUT another subject, administratively owned by the author because consolidate gives a derived fact the sources' owner, is **DENIED** — and asserted denied even as the ONLY fact present, so the denial is provably the derivatives rule and not the cap firing. **And the limit of that exclusion:** a derived fact whose SUBJECT is the requester is **ALLOWED**, because the exclusion is scoped to the owner permit, not to derived facts in general — without that case, deriving-then-hiding a member's own inference would read as compliance. **FAULT TWINS, EXECUTED BOTH DIRECTIONS:** owner_permit_cap is a parameter, so the pre-R12 behaviour is directly reachable — raised out of the way, all ten cross-subject facts are admitted, the exact cross-report file R12 forbids; at cap=0 nothing cross-subject is admitted **while the author's own record is STILL fully admitted**, proving the self-exemption is STRUCTURAL rather than an artifact of the default value. ANTI-VACUITY: the baseline fixture is proven admissible at all (else cases asserting on 'what was admitted' would pass while checking nothing), the cap default is asserted against the live signature so a change must be deliberate, and the denial-reason string is pinned. **THE NAMED LIMIT SURVIVES MET, recorded explicitly:** R12's section 6.5 limit is unchanged — **the author's retention of their own ciphertext is entrenched by the DEK wrap**, removing it is a re-encryption not a policy edit — so **R12 caps what ELSE an author receives (corroboration, derivatives, aggregation, profile) and NEVER their own sentence.** A12 can never assert the author loses what they themselves stored. TD-110's cross-member WRITE gap is untouched: R12 and INJ-3 are read-side. **THE SECTION-16 PREAMBLE, STALE THREE TIMES (D-88, D-92, D-100), REWORKED NOT PATCHED:** now a **MET / NOT-MET split** (MET: R1 D-100, R12 D-103, R29 D-76, R30 D-94; NOT MET: R10 D-100, R18 D-88) rather than a bare list of 'ruled' requirements — **because a bare list cannot go stale VISIBLY: it stays true while becoming useless.** Updated in the same edit as the ruling it enumerates. D-92's direction ruling retained in full beneath the MET entry (the record of WHY the architecture moved instead of the requirement is worth more than the outcome), its now-superseded 'A12 STAYS CONTRADICTED' line annotated rather than rewritten. EVIDENCE: 17 batteries **258 passed / 8 xfailed**; AUDIT 8/8, DISC 1/1, L7 27/27, **L7V2 27/28 as required**, SCHEMA 1/1, VOICE 1/1, **RATCHET PASS, 0 scenario FAILs, all five ABSOLUTE read individually** (G0, PSA1, CTX-STRIP, LI1, CS1). **MUTATION SELF-TEST STILL FINDS ITS MUTANT** as required: negate_if_test @ injection_contract.py:664, killed_with_full_killers=True survives_with_killers_excluded=True — D-102's resync holds. **MEMORY HARNESS 13/17, the identical four** (MEM-115/116/117/118) — zero delta, no STOP. --full refused by TD-129 |
| D-105 dispatch: TD-139 partially closed — R18's lineage block, 4 of 11 implemented | docs/techdebt/LATEST_DEBT.md (TD-139) + eval/test_lineage_block.py | PARTIAL | D-105, 2026-08-02. **Nothing ruled MET. R18 STAYS NOT MET regardless — TD-140 (recompute) is still open and awaiting Bill's ruling; TD-141 untouched; NO cascade behaviour changed.** Reconciled-Against 4832ef9. **SURVEY FIRST, and the split IS the work — what is NOT written matters as much as what is, because a lineage field filled with a guess is worse than an absent one.** **IMPLEMENTED (4)**, at create_fact_node, scoped to DERIVED artifacts (R18's own scope: 'every derived artifact') and FAIL-CLOSED — a derived write missing one is REFUSED with no write issued: `artifact_type` (CREATOR-stamped 'fact'; a caller-supplied value is OVERWRITTEN, since provenance a caller can forge is not provenance), `audience_policy` (write_class.visibility — **genuinely new information**: dyad_id and recipient_ref were stored, the CLASS was not), `derivation_method` ('consolidate.abstract.v1', versioned so a second derivation path cannot arrive unlabelled), `source_categories` (**the PARENTS' attributes, read from the parent rows** — DerivedFact carries parent IDs but not their attributes, so this needed one plumbing hop from _abstract_pass, which has the episodes in scope). **ALREADY PRESENT UNDER ANOTHER NAME (4) — deliberately NOT duplicated, because a second copy of a stored fact is DRIFT, not lineage (TD-137's lesson applied to provenance):** artifact_id == fact_id; parent_artifact_ids == derived_from; sensitivity == sensitivity; subjects ~ subject (consolidate groups episodes BY subject, so a derived fact has exactly one). **This corrects TD-139's own original '2 of 11' count** — three were already stored, counting by R18's field list. **STILL OPEN (3), each with what it would need:** `purpose_id` — **no purpose vocabulary exists anywhere**, verified ABSENT across harness/ and memory_engine/; needs R23's purpose triggers first. `retention_deadline` — **no retention mechanism exists**, R21 NOT MET, and this field cannot precede it. `policy_version` — **none exists, and relabelling sensitivity_registry_version as one would be a LIE in a provenance field**, exactly what R18's block must never contain. All three asserted ABSENT by a standing test so a placeholder cannot be added quietly. **NOT BACKFILLED, asserted not assumed:** no backfill was ordered, and unlike R30 item 5's pre-registry marker there is no ruling on what a pre-lineage artifact should say. The one live derived fact never passed through the creator as derived at all — demo_seed SETs `derived=true` post-hoc after an `encode` write — so it is **TD-141's shape, not a failure of this gate**. ACCEPTANCE: eval/test_lineage_block.py, **16 cases**, registered in run_harness.sh — fields asserted on a real derived write through consolidate's OWN writer (not a hand-built dict, which would only prove a dict contains what I put in it), a parametrized fault twin per required field, blank-string and empty-list treated as missing, non-derived writes proven unaffected, deliberate absences asserted, no-duplicate asserted in both directions. **THREE THINGS THE HARNESS CAUGHT IN MY OWN WORK, all fixed:** (1) CEIL-CONV's frozen fixtures correctly detected the stored-shape change; updated to **fixtures v3** with the delta established by before/after diff and confirmed **ADDED-only — nothing removed, nothing changed**. (2) My first fixture edit added artifact_type to EVERY map including `encode_props` — **wrong: encode_props is BUILDER-level and must not carry creator-stamped fields, the same reason it carries no `origin`**; the battery caught the distinction and it is now asserted both ways. (3) The L7 OB4 sealing probe called _write_derived_node without source_categories and the gate refused it — a legitimate caller needing plumbing, fixed and disclosed (it tests key_version=2, not lineage), same class as D-97's OB4 fix. An AST-exhaustive sweep then confirmed **zero** unplumbed callers remain. EVIDENCE: 19 batteries **293 passed / 8 xfailed**; AUDIT 8/8, DISC 1/1, L7 27/27, **L7V2 27/28 as required**, SCHEMA 1/1, VOICE 1/1, **RATCHET PASS, 0 scenario FAILs, all five ABSOLUTE read individually** (G0, PSA1, CTX-STRIP, LI1, CS1). **Mutation self-test still finds its mutant at injection_contract.py:664** as required (killed_with_full_killers=True survives_with_killers_excluded=True). **MEMORY HARNESS 13/17, the identical four** — and MEM-111 (derived tagging) still PASSES, which is end-to-end proof that consolidation's real derived write satisfies the new gate. --full refused by TD-129 |
| Ledger head anchoring: detectability, not resistance (BOTH HALVES BUILT AND EXERCISED — D-98) | design/DESIGN_LEDGER_ANCHOR__detectability-not-resistance__v20260801_1300.md | BUILT | D-89, 2026-08-01. **Rules NOTHING MET. Buys DETECTABILITY, NOT RESISTANCE — nothing here stops a rewrite, it makes one VISIBLE to someone who held an earlier head. Custody separation is a different, later build and this is not a step toward it.** Reconciled-Against 6698a49. Authority REQ_ARCHITECTURE_BOUNDARY section 5.2. THE PROBLEM, re-verified not recalled: `ledger/` and `ledger/keys` are bill-ai:staff **uid 501** — the uid every HIP process runs as — so any of them can rewrite from genesis and re-hash, and epistemic_ledger.verify() still returns True because it reads the chain it is supposed to distrust. **WHAT:** chain head only — anchor_version/seq/head_hash/segment-basename/event_count/anchored_at, enforced by ANCHOR_FIELDS + anchor_leaks() + a serializer that REFUSES a leaky record. No payloads, no member ids, no attribute names, and deliberately **no actor** (actor.id is plaintext in the immortal envelope per HEL-ACTOR-1, so copying the tail event through would leak a member id). **WHERE: STOPPED — the ambiguity, assessed not assumed.** No cloud credentials exist (~/.aws, ~/.config/gcloud, ~/.azure all absent); tailscale CLI unavailable so no second device is reachable or credentialed; a printed/mailed digest is viable but is an operator process needing Bill's decision; and the git remote **FAILS THE CRITERION ON THE MERITS** — HIP's own uid holds push credentials (this session pushed with them all day), so it is writable by exactly the uid the anchor must be protected from, and it is not append-only. **No target satisfying 'not writable by the UID that runs HIP' exists on this box today.** **RESOLVED (D-90, same day): Bill ruled the target is his laptop, PULL not push, no credential to it in either direction. BUILT AND EXERCISED (D-95's spec, D-98's execution): rsync --ignore-existing pulled from the laptop, laptop-initiated; two anchors at seq 7074 held read-only (444) on the laptop; a SECOND fetch transferred nothing, live-proving append-only rather than merely intending it; a ledger mirror pulled hel-*.jsonl only, ledger/keys/ excluded and its absence verified afterward; the verifier was cloned from GitHub, not the mini; both anchors verify MATCH against head_hash sha256:140ea35e...ab8a5e, independently re-confirmed from this box against the mini's own drawer files.** **THE SIGNING KEY IS THE SAME QUESTION — the important finding.** On one box with one uid, any key the signer can read the core can read, so a HIP-held signing key is forgeable by precisely the adversaries anchoring exists to detect. **Resolution: HIP does not sign at all** — it emits an unsigned record and the RECEIVER countersigns and timestamps, so evidentiary weight comes from the receiver's possession at a point in time rather than a secret HIP holds. build_anchor() is deliberately signature-free. WHERE and key custody are ONE decision, stopped together. **HOW OFTEN:** daily + on-demand around operator actions, recommended not ruled. **The interval IS the detection window** — a rewrite between anchors is undetectable forever; not every-event because the receiver would become a hard dependency on the write path, conflicting with the D-1 invariant. An anchor SET gives two bounds: oldest bounds how far back a rewrite reached, newest bounds unprotected recent history. **VERIFICATION:** verify_against_anchor() needs no key, no HIP process, no payload access — only segments + the record. Two checks: internal consistency (recompute every hash, check prev_hash links) AND the anchor comparison. **A competent rewriter passes the first**, which is exactly why the second exists and why the ledger's own verify() is insufficient. Truncation caught separately. A mismatch RETURNS rather than raises — **a mismatch is evidence, not an error.** **CRYPTO-SHRED CONSTRAINT — PROVEN, NOT ASSERTED.** The chain hashes payload_sha256 (a digest over CIPHERTEXT), and `payload`/`payload_enc` and `erased` are all deliberately OUTSIDE _HASH_FIELDS. So: destroy_member_key touches NO segment file (shreds the key, appends a system.note); erase_payload nulls content IN PLACE leaving hash/payload_sha256/prev_hash intact. **Neither erasure path mutates any past event — both only APPEND.** Measured on a temp ledger: baseline seq=4, after shred seq=5, after tombstone seq=6, verify True throughout, and **the tombstoned event was seq 2 — inside the checked prefix — with its own hash UNCHANGED**, which is the decisive observation. Consequence: **an anchor at (seq N, head H) stays valid forever**, however many lawful erasures follow. Shown to be a property of the DESIGN not luck — a fault twin proves a chain hashing payload_enc directly WOULD change hash on tombstone, making lawful erasure indistinguishable from tampering. **BUILT:** harness/ledger_anchor.py (build_anchor, anchor_leaks, anchor_to_line, verify_against_anchor/s) + eval/test_ledger_anchor.py, **14 cases**, registered in run_harness.sh (12 batteries). All three acceptance criteria with fault twins: a rewrite-from-genesis is DETECTED (twin: a blind verifier — the ledger's own verify() — MUST accept the rewrite, or the real check is passing for the wrong reason); a lawful shred and a lawful tombstone are NOT flagged; the record leaks no canary (**anti-vacuity proves the canaries were really in the ledger**, else the leak test would pass against an anchor leaking everything). **NOT BUILT AT TIME OF WRITING (D-89, morning): the writer-to-target and any local signer** — a local signer with a locally-readable key would look like a control while being forgeable by the adversaries it names, the exact trap the dispatch warned about, and still the reason no local signer exists. **UPDATE (D-90+D-95+D-98, same day): THE WRITER-TO-TARGET IS NOW BUILT ON BOTH ENDS AND EXERCISED** — see the resolution note above. No local signer was built, but that was never the plan; the design was always receiver-countersigns, not HIP-signs. **What is still missing is the countersignature itself: the laptop holds anchors append-only and read-only but does not yet cryptographically countersign or timestamp them — evidentiary weight today rests on the laptop's unsigned possession alone.** **SO: REQ_ARCHITECTURE_BOUNDARY section 5.2 recorded 'an audit anchor the writer cannot reach' as a property that did not exist. IT NOW DOES**, with the countersignature gap above as the honest limit of what 'exists' means, not glossed over — **this buys DETECTABILITY, not RESISTANCE, and the interval between anchors is the detection window, permanently: a rewrite of events appended between two anchors (or after the newest one held) is undetectable by this scheme, forever.** HARNESS: 12 batteries 162 passed / 9 xfailed; AUDIT 8/8, L7 27/27, L7V2 27/28, RATCHET PASS, 0 scenario FAILs, all five ABSOLUTE PASS read individually. --full refused by TD-129 as expected. **Battery hermeticity VERIFIED not claimed** (it calls destroy_member_key): every case builds its own ledger under tmp_path via HIP_HEL_DIR; the real chain is intact at 7074 events with verify True and 0 tombstoned, and the real member_maya.key still carries its Jul 18 20:22 mtime. **REMAINING OPEN ITEMS (D-98): cadence is still NOT SET (daily is a recommendation, not a ruling) and NO LAUNCHD JOB IS INSTALLED ON EITHER MACHINE** — both the mini's emission and the laptop's fetch were run BY HAND for this proof, so the detection window today is bounded only by how often a human remembers to run both steps; whether a countersignature is required for v1 (above); and whether submission may ever block a turn (recommended NO — it would conflict with the D-1 invariant, unaffected by today's update) |
| Earned calibration design note: validated correctness, not usage (filed against TD-140) | design/DESIGN_EARNED_CALIBRATION__validated-correctness-not-usage__v20260801_1214.md | PLAN | D-88, 2026-08-01. **A DESIGN NOTE, NOT A REQUIREMENT — proposes no requirement and no status, authorizes no code; CLAUDE.md item 8's gate is NOT satisfied by it for any build.** Reconciled-Against b88e629. Filed against TD-140 (the R18 recompute branch never executes). PURPOSE: capture Bill's idea WITH ITS KNOWN COLLISION ALREADY ATTACHED so whoever picks it up does not rediscover it the hard way. THE IDEA: early on, retracting one source should KILL the derived fact; over time HIP should REBUILD from surviving parents, governed by a system-level confidence measure that starts small because HIP is new and grows as the household uses it. **THE COLLISION, STATED FIRST:** a measure keyed on USAGE re-imports the engagement-earns-depth defect the ceiling ruling eliminated — REQ_STRUCTURAL_CEILING's control rule is that engagement may justify OFFERING a deeper capability but may NEVER itself AUTHORIZE deeper collection, and the population most at risk emits no negative signal at all: the compliant, lonely, deferential, and cognitively declining household uses HIP MOST and would earn the MOST inferential latitude. 'Grows as the household uses it' is that same signal wearing different clothes. Not a reason to abandon the idea — a reason to key it on something else. **THE DEFENSIBLE VERSION:** key it on VALIDATED CORRECTNESS, not usage. Candidate signals, all about HIP's derived OUTPUT rather than member behavior: confirm rate on HIP's own derived facts, correction rate, and survival of derived facts through retraction cascades — measured against ground truth HIP DOES NOT CONTROL (member corrections and confirmations are statements about the world HIP did not author, which is what keeps this from being self-certification). THE DISCRIMINATING CONSEQUENCE: a household that uses HIP constantly but corrects it half the time earns **LESS** latitude, not more — the inversion is the test of whether a proposed signal is the right kind. **THE SCOPE LINE THAT MAKES IT BUILDABLE:** the measure MAY gate INFERENTIAL RECOVERY — whether an ALREADY EXISTING derived fact survives when a parent is retracted — which sits inside the ceiling because the recovered fact is the same fact it already was, with categories, audience and retention all still binding; nothing new is collected and nothing widens. It SHALL NOT gate COLLECTION DEPTH (no new categories, subjects, audiences, retention, or inferential reach), which the control rule forbids. FIVE OPEN QUESTIONS, unanswered by design: per-household vs per-inference-class (a single scalar means reliability about medication buys latitude on relationship inferences); **whether it can ever DECREASE** (flagged load-bearing — if it cannot, it is a ratchet on inferential latitude, which is what the ceiling exists to prevent); what stops it becoming an optimization target (**the evaluator-is-not-optimizer house rule applies**, and the note names the direct tension with A27/R27 since a confirm-rate measure is structurally adjacent to a forbidden acceptance-rate objective); cold start (the floor IS today's unconditional-invalidate behavior, so TD-140's current state is this design's floor rather than a departure from it); and whose correction counts (subject vs author vs unrelated member are not equivalent evidence). Cross-refs TD-139/TD-140/TD-141, R18 and its section-16 ruling, the control rule, A27/R27, and REQ_ARCHITECTURE_BOUNDARY section 4 — a recompute is a model-authored write, so models-propose/core-commits binds anything built here |
| Architecture boundary: reference monitor, threat model, contracted clients | requirements/REQ_ARCHITECTURE_BOUNDARY__reference-monitor-threat-model-and-contracted-clients__v20260801_0919.md | FILED | D-85, 2026-08-01. **FILED, acceptance NOT run, nothing MET, no code authorized (its own section 7 says so).** Reconciled-Against 4ae70cc (verified by READING HEAD at filing time, not a remembered hash). RECORDS RULINGS ALREADY MADE, not new design. Evidence: the two D-84 artifacts banked verbatim this dispatch, both UNVERIFIED. **THREAT MODEL** in scope T1 careless code, T2 hostile household member, T3 compromised dependency, T4 remote attacker; T5 THE OPERATOR is OUT OF SCOPE FOR NOW, **deferred not dismissed** — whoever holds OS root defeats every boundary in the document, which bounds what the ledger can be credible TO. THE CONSEQUENCE STATED PLAINLY: **three of the four in-scope adversaries defeat an in-process boundary in CPython** (module privacy is a naming convention, annotations are erased at runtime, reflection and monkeypatching reach everything, a capability token is forgeable by anything sharing its address space, an admission proof HMAC'd with a key the forger can read is circular); **careless code is the ONLY one an in-process structural boundary stops** — which is exactly and only what A10 buys. Everything else needs the OS boundary: separate process, own UID, a credential the others do not have. **MONOLITH RULING RATIFIED** — core stays monolithic inside a hard boundary with contracted clients outside; gates are ordered and interdependent, splitting the chain multiplies omission surfaces, and there is no scale pressure at all. Named for what it is: a **REFERENCE MONITOR** with **COMPLETE MEDIATION** as the target property (vocabulary adopted from the banked ChatGPT pass). **Monolithic is the deployment shape; complete mediation is the security property; the first does not imply the second — adopting the name is adopting the obligation.** AND IT DOES NOT HAVE THAT PROPERTY TODAY, recorded not scheduled: ~14 modules reach a graph write, **at least 9 outside any defensible boundary**, all by import; **three independent :Fact-CREATE implementations** (store.encode, extraction_queue._write_one:81, consolidate._write_derived_node:54) kept in agreement by COMMENT DISCIPLINE, with a fourth older variant running live from the frozen tree; the **plist credential fallback lets any process self-serve the graph password** and memory_dashboard.py:40 imports that accessor directly; **Neo4j Community has no row-level access control**, so the credential IS the entire wall and N processes holding it is not a smell but the hole; ordering is enforced three different ways, and the proof the read chain is convention rather than structure is that **INJ-7 is disabled by default** (member_ids=None) so a caller omitting one kwarg silently drops a gate. **VOICE BOUNDARY** — voice is UNTRUSTED BY CONSTRUCTION regardless of vendor; HIP does not secure the voice stack, the boundary holds whatever is on the other side. Two hard rules: voice NEVER holds a graph credential; voice NEVER executes in the process that owns writes. The turn/on_route/register_member/session_end contract is hereby a **SECURITY boundary, not merely a modularity one**. THE LIVE DEPLOYMENT IS THIS EXACTLY INVERTED, verified against launchd not memory: the voice orch runs from the FROZEN hip-harness checkout, so every roadmap gate improvement since the freeze (R29/R30, the R18 cascade, fail-closed sensitivity, the trust fixes) is ABSENT from the process actually taking audio — and that same process holds the graph credential, the extraction write path, the HEL append, AND the memory-unsafe parsers (Whisper/Kokoro/resemblyzer) in one heap. Both hard rules violated today. **INFERENCE BOUNDARY** — model output is attacker-influenced input and HIP's can write; **MODELS PROPOSE, ONLY THE CORE COMMITS**, stated as a contract DIRECTION authorizing no code. Two model-output-to-write paths exist (extraction via Ollama; frontier return via write_frontier_fact -> encode) and **neither has an authority gate — TD-110**. Premise correction carried: the model CALL already crosses a process boundary everywhere (Ollama HTTP, Groq/OpenAI remote), so the exposure is the two write paths, egress (TD-131, strip_context_for_tier not covering MID/CORE), and the voice process's shared heap — NOT the call site. **THE TWO PROPERTIES THE SECURITY STORY RESTS ON THAT DO NOT EXIST:** (1) a single-writer process that actually exists — the ruling describes an intended architecture, not a deployed one; three dashboards were running at recon time, two able to import process_text_query and run a full governed turn in-process; (2) an audit anchor the writer cannot reach — the HEL is strong within its trust model (hash-chained, F_FULLFSYNC before reply, per-member crypto-shred, chain hash over ciphertext, flock'd ordering) and has **no structural separation whatsoever**: same UID, so any process can destroy_member_key, delete segments, or rewrite the chain from genesis and re-hash, with verification reading the same disk it distrusts. **A hash chain custodied by the process that writes it is tamper-evident against nobody.** Also recorded: audit is best-effort BY DESIGN (append never raises toward the caller — the D-1 invariant), so a turn whose record failed still answers. **Anchoring (detectable) and separation (hard) are different properties and NOT substitutes; Bill has now built the cheap one (below), which is a fact about what exists, not a formal ruling foreclosing the expensive one — whether separation is ever also built remains open.** **UPDATE (D-98, 2026-08-01), stated with its limit in the same breath: property (2) NOW EXISTS.** A second machine (Bill's laptop) HIP holds no credential to and cannot initiate toward now holds (seq, head_hash) records fetched at its own initiative (D-90 mini emitter, D-95 laptop spec, D-98 built and exercised); two anchors at seq 7074 verified from this box to carry head_hash sha256:140ea35e...ab8a5e. **This buys DETECTABILITY, NOT RESISTANCE — nothing stops the rewrite described above, and the interval between anchors is the detection window, permanently: a rewrite between two anchors, or after the newest one held, is undetectable by this scheme forever.** Open: cadence not set, no launchd job on either machine (both steps run by hand for this proof), receiver countersignature unbuilt so evidentiary weight rests on the laptop's unsigned possession alone. Full detail: DESIGN_LEDGER_ANCHOR row above. Cross-refs TD-110, TD-131, A10 (**whose strict-xfail was never written** — same correction A18 needed at D-81; encode performs none of R10's four checks), ledger anchoring (not filed as a TD — the anchor half is now built, so no defect exists to file; custody separation remains the unmade, unbuilt ruling), TD-137, REQ_VOICE_COMPONENT. Flags the D-82 recon as still UNBANKED. Companion backlog item #54. Symlink LATEST_REQ_ARCHITECTURE_BOUNDARY.md |

So the build must NOT 'wire G3'; G3 runs. **The real gap is a DIRECTION, and it is structural:** G3's first line (`if not r.get('guard_triggered'): return None`, record_invariants.py:113) short-circuits to clean whenever no guard fired, so G3 asserts only **guard fired => no inference ran**. The converse — **an asserted refusal => a guard fired** — is asserted by NOTHING. (2) a refusal passes only when inference_ms is **NULL, not falsy** — an inference_ms=0 refusal must FAIL, the exact bug record_invariants.py:116 already carries a fix comment for; (3) classify_refusal may remain for REPORTING but may not decide pass/fail; (4) **FAULT TWIN, executed**: refusal-shaped prose with the gate not fired must PASS today and FAIL after, removing the violation returns green; (5) the existing Layer-4 suite still passes on unmodified main and **any row that goes red is reported as a FINDING, not fixed silently** — a row green only because text-matching accepted a model-produced refusal is a real defect this change surfaces; (6) REQ_HARNESS_DISCIPLINE's four parts, each stated so they cannot be met nominally (twin executed both directions; hand-authored fixtures incl. the inference_ms=0 boundary; coverage entry with NAMED UNCOVERED; **metamorphic wrapper = a meaning-preserving rewording must not change pass/fail**, which is the whole point and flips to fail under today's text grading). **WHAT'S ALREADY DONE, do not rebuild:** G3 itself (correct, with the falsy fix in-source), its twins (harness_audit.py:183-185) and mutation killers (mutation_targets.py:644-655), the record-grading pattern already proven in-tree on ONE assertion on the write path (layer4.py:117-137, with its own comment citing D-16), the reusable record reader _last_record_for, and every record field needed (epistemic_record.py:184/227/313/265). **CONSTRAINTS:** classify_refusal and its regexes STAY (the F-4 ordering at server.py:116-117 is a real property); **G3 is not to be modified** — this REQ adds the converse direction, it does not touch the direction that works; no schema change to epistemic_record (**a build that widens the record to make the check easier has changed the instrument to fit the measurement**); RATCHET and the five ABSOLUTE checks hold; and row 5 is a HARD constraint — **a check adjusted until it passes is the failure mode this requirement exists to remove.** OUT OF SCOPE, named so a build cannot quietly widen: Layer 1/2 refusal assertions (still text-graded, and the coverage entry must say so), the `value` outcome, two-sided scoring / the opposite-polarity ratchet (backlog 38, blocked on BILL-6), disclosure_oracle's wiring status, and gen_pairwise probe generation. Related: D-16 (backlog 12, same conflation from the reporting side), TD-132 (paraphrase limit, same class from the leak side). **NO MANIFEST ROW — checked against the file and the law, not assumed, and this is a DEVIATION FROM THE DISPATCH, flagged rather than silently either way.** Voice 37 asked for MANIFEST registration. roadmap's docs/deliverables/MANIFEST.md carries **ZERO** non-deliverable rows (0 matches for ../requirements|dispatches|reviews|design/) and **ZERO** REQ rows — including REQ_STRUCTURAL_CEILING, REQ_CEILING_ACCEPTANCE and REQ_ARCHITECTURE_BOUNDARY, i.e. every major REQ of this session. Symlink LATEST_REQ_RECORD_GRADED_REFUSAL.md **BUILD LANDED 2026-08-02 (Voice 37 build).** layer4.py::_structural_refusal + _guard_kind: the refusal outcomes (access_control, empty_set, no_leak) now resolve on the RECORD; classify_refusal retained for the detail string ONLY. eval/test_record_graded_refusal.py, 19 cases, registered in run_harness.sh. **The twin was proven PASS BEFORE the change and FAIL AFTER**, green again when the violation is removed — all three directions executed, not described. **TWO GENUINE FINDINGS, accepted by Bill (2026-08-02) and recorded as KNOWN REDS via the sanctioned --update-baseline --accept path (D-17), each with its own defect ID and one-line note in `_accepted` — visible on the board, not buried:** **TD-143** (L4:PW010, sam->ray/allergy [not-exists]) and **TD-144** (L4:PW015, sam->dad/medication [exists-cross-member]). Under text grading both were GREEN. **This is Bill's sentence made executable: 'a refusal the model was told to produce' counted as 'a refusal where no model ran'.** **TD-144 IS THE SERIOUS ONE and is filed as its own defect needing its own REQ: a cross-member fact that EXISTS is protected only by the model's cooperation, no gate fires** — the exact inverse of the product claim that the model cannot decline to reveal what it never saw. The two failures have non-member subjects (ray, dad) and expect empty_set, i.e. INJ-6/INJ-6b territory, interacting with TD-120 D2 (relation->name mapping not built, so 'Dad' never resolves to a graph subject). **The hole is in the EMPTY-SET guard for non-member subjects**, and establishing how wide it is is the first job of TD-144's REQ. **TWO ERRORS OF MINE, both caught by the harness, both fixed, both recorded:** (1) I read a top-level `guard_kind`; **the emitted field is NESTED at `guard.kind` and 0 of 43 live records carry a top-level `guard_kind`** — `guard_kind` is the PARAMETER name at epistemic_record.py:184. Cost: a full red Layer-4 run, 13 rows, indistinguishable on its face from real findings — **and unit fixtures that were GREEN AGAINST A RECORD SHAPE THAT DOES NOT EXIST**, which is the worse half. Both rebuilt from a real record, with an anti-vacuity case asserting the fixture's shape against the live log so it cannot recur. **The REQ's own WHAT'S ALREADY DONE line caused this and was CORRECTED IN PLACE per Bill's instruction**, with a field table verified against 43 records and the lesson stated: build fixtures from a REAL RECORD, never from the emitter's signature — a parameter name and an emitted key are different things, and a fixture derived from the signature agrees with itself forever. (2) I registered the battery in check_registry.py; the four-part roster audit **correctly rejected it as a stale entry** — zero pytest batteries live there (D-75's A29/A30 precedent), they register in run_harness.sh. The four reasons are IN the requirement, per the ruling: D-104 proved the original derivation is a live Groq call, so recompute-from-survivors is a SECOND NON-DETERMINISTIC MODEL CALL with no stored recipe to re-run; a rule describing an operation the system cannot perform reads as a commitment and is not one; invalidate-only errs toward FORGETTING MORE than the old clause required, never less (lost inference, not retained inference); and recompute-as-fresh-inference is NOT foreclosed — it returns as its own REQ through the inference-permit machinery if ever wanted. **TD-140 RESOLVED by requirement change, not by a build** (register v20260802_2115). A18's acceptance row reworded to match ("invalidates every dependent child" — what the passing battery always asserted). `cascade_recompute_eligible`/`cascade_recompute_from` stay written-and-unread as breadcrumbs per the door-open clause; derivation_cascade.py's docstring updated to quote the amended rule (comment-only). **R18's MET status deliberately NOT re-ruled — section 16 records the evidence both ways for Bill** (toward MET: amended operative rule built and live-proven, A18 LIVE+passing, TD-139/140/141 all resolved or closed; toward caution: purpose_id/retention_deadline/policy_version deliberately absent pending R21/R23, storage-class erase still weak-sense only, 12 pre-lineage facts unruled for durable graphs). PRIOR: **RULED 2026-08-01 (D-76), recorded in the REQ's new section 16 — Bill's calls, never self-marked. Only R18, R29 and R30 are ruled; every other requirement remains FILED with acceptance NOT run.** **R18 NOT MET (Bill's ruling, D-88, 2026-08-01).** The D-81 cascade (4ae70cc) is real and correct as far as it goes — retracting a parent closes every derived descendant to a fixpoint, in the caller's transaction, children stamped closed_by='lineage_cascade' — but it does not satisfy R18. **TD-140 IS THE REQUIREMENT GAP:** R18 states a two-branch rule, recompute-then-invalidate, and **only the else branch exists** — nothing consumes cascade_recompute_eligible/cascade_recompute_from, so every cascaded child is invalidated and none is ever recomputed. That is not a partial implementation of recompute; it is the absence of recompute with a note attached. **THE BUILT HALF ERRS TOWARD LESS INFERENCE, WHICH IS THE SAFE DIRECTION TO FAIL** — a child that could have been rebuilt is destroyed instead of kept, so the system forgets MORE than R18 requires, never less, and R18's closing sentence (a child SHALL NOT remain active merely because another parent still exists) holds strictly. The failure mode is lost inference, not retained inference. Remaining scope, all three carried at 4ae70cc: **TD-139** the lineage block is 2 of 11 fields (a :Fact carries derived and derived_from, parent_artifact_ids only by rename), so 'erase according to its storage class' holds only in the weak sense that closing removes from retrieval — nothing erases by storage class because none is recorded; **TD-140** above, deliberate in its ORDERING (re-deriving needs an LLM call and a timeout inside the retraction path would leave the child ACTIVE, exactly what R18 forbids, so the child closes first and unconditionally — the design choice is defensible, the requirement is still unmet); **TD-141** the cascade is correct but INERT — the live graph's one derived fact has an EMPTY derived_from (D-81's read-only pre-change inventory: 12 :Fact nodes, 1 derived=true, **0 with lineage populated**, 0 retracted-parent children), so the defect had never fired, and the reason is that no lineage was ever written, which is its own violation of R18's opening sentence. **'The cascade passes its battery' and 'the graph is protected' are separate claims.** ACCEPTANCE NOTE: A18 PASSES (eval/test_derivation_cascade.py, 4ae70cc, 20 cases, in run_harness.sh) and D-86 recorded its declared STRICT XFAIL tier as stale — **that changes nothing**, because a passing acceptance row does not carry its requirement, the same shape as A30 passing while R30 is NOT MET. Design note filed against TD-140: design/DESIGN_EARNED_CALIBRATION__validated-correctness-not-usage__v20260801_1214.md (a note, not a REQ). **R29 MET.** Evidence: one registry at harness/sensitivity.py (LOW=10/MEDIUM=20/HIGH=30/CRITICAL=40, order authoritative); four consumers importing it with no local enum or ordering default surviving (curator_shadow's shared _ORDINAL split so confidence keeps its own vocabulary; hipconfig's SENSITIVITY_RANK deleted; extraction_queue's SENSITIVITY_LEVELS now an alias OBJECT not a second literal; permissions' _HIGH_SENSITIVITY derived from the order); unknown values failing closed at every boundary (rank() has no default parameter by construction); A29+A30 passing at ABSOLUTE tier (31 cases, batteries 44->75); and L7:CS1 HELD -- which mattered because the change alters the sensitivity FEATURE VALUE inside a MET component whose ABSOLUTE check asserts the exact ten keys. --layer 7 exit 0, all 5 ABSOLUTE PASS, AUDIT 8/8, RATCHET PASS, zero FAIL lines. Commits d50225e and c58167e. **R30 MET (D-94, 2026-08-01).** Items 1-4 complete (inventory ran BEFORE any change and found zero unmapped values across Neo4j 12/12, the record log, and config; migrate/quarantine vacuous; unknown rejected at every write boundary; cross-module ordering tests added). **ITEM 5 IS NOW BUILT (D-93, b4ea004): SENSITIVITY_REGISTRY_VERSION IS STAMPED, NOT MERELY EXPOSED.** All THREE :Fact write paths stamp it, each importing the constant rather than carrying a literal -- store.py::_new_node_props/_CREATE_FACT_CQL, extraction_queue.py::_write_one, consolidate.py::_write_derived_node (D-84 established :Fact is CREATEd in three independent places; stamping one path only would have made 'a fact with no marker is impossible' false the next time either other path ran). PRE_REGISTRY and VERSION_VALUES live beside SENSITIVITY_REGISTRY_VERSION in harness/sensitivity.py so the marker and the version it marks against cannot drift apart. The migration is APPLIED not lazy, and idempotent by construction (guarded WHERE sensitivity_registry_version IS NULL, so it can never relabel an already-stamped fact) -- 12 facts were pre-registry before, 0 unstamped after. An 18-case battery (CEIL-RV, one fault twin per row) is now the 14th standing battery, caught a bug in itself on first run (its own AST predicate missed two paths embedding the field inside a Cypher string, fixed before being trusted), all five ABSOLUTE read individually green, RATCHET PASS, 0 scenario FAILs, and ORTH-2's 46-case fact-schema battery passed undisturbed despite the new :Fact property. **THE LIMITATION, part of this ruling, not a footnote:** acceptance row 1 ('a new fact carries the current version') is proven by the props builder plus an EXPLAIN against live Neo4j with the parameter bound, NOT by an executed end-to-end write -- the layer-7 run wrote no new facts and D-93 declined to write a probe fact into the demo graph to manufacture one. EXPLAIN proves the Cypher parses and the parameter binds, so the property cannot be silently dropped from the query plan; it does not prove execution semantics, that a real write under real concurrency actually persists the value. Row 1 becomes fully proven the first time a real turn writes a fact; the check to re-run then is the same version-distribution query that found 12 pre-registry / 0 unstamped above. **WHY THIS IS MET WHILE R18, RULED THE SAME DAY, IS NOT MET, so the pair is not read as inconsistent:** here every required behavior exists and works and one confirmation (a live end-to-end write) has not been observed; in R18 a required branch genuinely does not execute -- nothing consumes cascade_recompute_eligible, so R18's recompute half has no code behind it in any sense, observed or not. Mechanism-complete-but-unobserved (R30) is not mechanism-absent (R18) -- the rulings differ because the underlying facts differ, not because the same evidence was judged inconsistently. **WHY THE SPLIT WAS DELIBERATE, not an oversight (historical, D-92):** items 1-4 made the CURRENT ordering correct and enforced; item 5 makes a PAST decision attributable. Without it a stored fact could not be attributed to the sensitivity vocabulary in force when it was classified, and the moment a v2 registry exists -- which R29's 10/20/30/40 spacing exists specifically to permit -- every prior classification would have become unattributable. Ruling R30 MET on items 1-4 alone would have recorded that gap as closed while the artifact trail needed to close it did not yet exist; it now does. **ALSO RECORDED (section 16): D-75 closed THREE FAIL-OPEN PATHS, not one misranking.** TD-137 was reported as a misranking; it was, but it survived undetected because all three broken paths failed PERMISSIVELY so nothing ever went red. (1) Router threshold failed open in BOTH directions -- .get(tag,0) put an unknown tag below `low` so it never forced local, and .get(threshold,99) made an unknown threshold unreachable so it also never forced local. (2) permissions Rule 5's bare `in` test meant an unrecognized value was not in the set, so the deny never fired and the fact could be disclosed. (3) _coerce_fact silently coerced unrecognized sensitivity to `medium` AT THE WRITE BOUNDARY, so a `critical` fact arriving under a renamed level would have been STORED as `medium` -- a durable downgrade, found while aliasing SENSITIVITY_LEVELS and caught by NEITHER TD-137 nor the D-74 survey, both of which had read the same file and reported only the duplicate literal. It is the one with durable consequences and was the least visible. PRIOR: **UPDATED 2026-07-31 (D-71): supersedes the D-70 filing v20260731_2057.** Three changes, nothing else (verified by diff). (1) **R16 RULED — BOTH mechanisms, not a choice.** The chain carries opaque keyed commitments and operation metadata; the payloads live OFF-ledger under per-member keys. Erasure works two ways and each covers what the other cannot: absence from the log for the chain, cryptographic shredding for the payloads. **Stated explicitly as EXTENDING the ratified design, not reversing it** -- the original driver is **47 USC 551**, the Cable Act destruction mandate on operators, cited to its source at specs/EPISTEMIC_LEDGER__hel-oq2-backup-replica__v20260714_1615.md §3.1 ('Backup without a destruction mechanism creates operator liability: 47 USC 551 and the GDPR right-to-erasure reach every copy, including replicas') and §6.2's mechanism (rm the member key; every payload on the primary AND every backup becomes permanently unreadable, no copy enumeration, no segment touched). The chain-retained / payload-erasable property is preserved and relocated rather than dropped. COST NAMED: a ledger rebuild (today's ledger dual-writes encrypted payloads inline, harness/epistemic_ledger.py:13-18) plus a new off-ledger payload store with its own key management, backup path and erasure proof -- and **R17 becomes LOAD-BEARING rather than downstream**, because if active artifacts are not separately erasable, splitting payloads out of the chain buys nothing. R17 must land with R16, not after it. (2) **R12 REWORDED to a PROPAGATION cap, not a readback ban.** The author retains their own contribution and that is ENTRENCHED, not permitted -- they hold the DEK wrap, so removing it is a re-encryption rather than a policy edit; the NAMED LIMIT at §6.5 is carried forward unchanged. R12 now governs what else authorship drags along. It also records where current behaviour actually diverges, and CORRECTS this session's own D-70 step-5 survey, which overstated it: INJ-3's owner permit is CONSISTENT with the preserved readback, and diverges on exactly two clauses -- aggregation (the permit is per-fact and unbounded, so reading back every fact one ever stored about a subject reconstructs the forbidden cross-report file) and derivatives (consolidate.py gives a derived fact 'the same owner and subject as the sources', so a fact derived from the author's statements is OWNED by the author and the same permit reaches it). A12 cannot pass without bounding the owner permit or changing derived-fact ownership; neither is proposed here. (3) **FALSE-MET CORRECTED in the header Related line** -- it read `REQ_CARE_TEAM_READ_AUTH (MET)`, which is false and contradicted R14's own text in the same document. That REQ is NOT MET (its header :2, its INDEX status column, and no MET block anywhere in it), and its five-row acceptance has no executable form (D-68). RATIONALE: extends the D-50 portrait principle (two disagreeing portraits both kept, system renders no verdict) down to trust strength -- don't adjudicate self-report against corroboration, preserve both. 'The artifact exists' and 'the artifact is in the repo' are different claims; only the second closes a provenance gap. **RESTATED 2026-08-01 (D-55):** open item 1 (the shadow scorer's trust_rung feature, Fable's three options, none picked) restated verbatim in-doc for visibility; still Bill's call, not resolved -- symlink LATEST_REQ_TRUST_AXES.md |

## dispatches/

One doc per dispatch that produces a finding, a measurement, or a code
change — written as part of the work, not after. Covers what REQ docs don't:
analysis, tracing, measurement. NOT a substitute for a REQ doc; BUILD
dispatches reference theirs, ANALYSIS/MEASUREMENT dispatches may have
REQ: NONE. Check here before re-tracing an already-answered question. See
CLAUDE.md Requirements Discipline items 9-11.

Moved here VERBATIM — subject, path, status and reconciled-against cells unchanged — in their existing relative order, and APPENDED rather than interleaved so that no row already in this table changed position. The anchor that caused it was fixed at D-R-184 (CLAUDE.md Workflow item 3); this sweep is TD-R-164's remaining half. Order within the moved block is accretion order, not chronological — it is preserved as evidence of how the rows arrived, not asserted as a sort.

A session refused to widen a boundary, said so in writing, and the ruling followed the next morning; recorded because that pattern is invisible unless someone writes down that it worked.** **THREE frames, not four:** *"I'm taking X"* already reached governance at `bfb09df` through NC 25's take-verb, so it is TWINNED — and the twin checks the REASON STRING to prove it is still NC 25's code path, so a later reimplementation fails it. **THE TIER (Amendment 3 Q1, flagged for overrule): take/taking → (a) always, unchanged; on/started/stopped → (a) when corroborated by a dosage or lexicon word, else (b) AMBIGUOUS_WRITE — clarify, NO model, NO write.** (b) is the MORE restrictive destination, not the softer one: (a) parks the fact and asks, (b) writes nothing at all; NOT_MEDICAL is what constraint 2 forbids and is unreachable once an object survives the exclusion list. **CAPITALISATION IS DELIBERATELY NOT CORROBORATION — ruling 4 generalised:** it is a typing artifact ASR does not produce, so letting it decide the class would land *"I'm on Zervolol"* typed and *"i'm on zervolol"* spoken in different classes — the modality-dependent classification F6 measured for punctuation, arriving through a different door. **CONSTRAINT 1 ANSWERED STRUCTURALLY:** *"I stopped at the store"* is released because `at` is a preposition heading an adjunct, which also releases *"stopped by the pharmacy"* — medical vocabulary, still not a medication assertion — and *"stopped for gas"*. **TWO DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED WHILE BUILDING, both silent wrong answers:** `_NEGATION` missed *"I'm not on X"* (contracts to `'m not`, which `am not` never matched) and *"I have not started X"*, both reading as POSITIVE polarity — a false record of what a member said about their own medication; and the object slot read the first token, so *"I started a new job"* resolved to `new` and governed a career change. **P6.6 — TWO GREEN TWINS RETIRED BY THE RULING THEY WERE BUILT TO FORCE** (NC 15's boundary twin, NC 25's residual twin), both updated with their old assertions kept VERBATIM in the test body; they were the ONLY two that went red. **ACCEPTANCE: 63 new twins — constraint 1 = 20, frame family = 8, constraint 2 = 17, polarity = 9, constraint 3 = 6, neighbours unmoved = 3; 300 passed across all six NC suites; suite failure SET byte-identical at 41 entries (20F/753P → 20F/816P, and 816−753 = 63 exactly), single-variable at `bfb09df` with HEAD re-checked immediately before the after-run.** Graph 7693 has no listener; preflight OK + NOT BUSY. **4 findings, none blocking:** inverted question forms don't match the frames (safe direction — cannot become an assertion); **the THIRD-PERSON frame is still out of scope, the natural successor to NC25-F1 and offered the same way**; the accepted false-positive now has a name (*"I'm on Zoom"* → a clarification, not a park); and TD-R-194(b)'s bricked row on its fifth hand-edit, deliberately not re-filed. Reconciled-Against `0ee55fb`. |

| **FM 10 — unload the launchd `voice_https_orch` service (second kill)** | `dispatches/DISPATCH_FM10_UNLOAD_LAUNCHD_VOICE_ORCH__second-kill-launchd-was-the-reason-it-returned__v20260814_1630.md` | **BUILT** — service stopped and persistently disabled; plist left on disk | FM 10, 2026-08-14 16:27-16:30 MDT. Bill's authorization verbatim: **"Unload it."** **STOP ONLY** — not rebound to loopback, plist not deleted. **BEFORE:** pid **72415** listening on **`*:7860` — ALL INTERFACES** (started 09:27:36, reparented to launchd); **no established connections**, so nothing was torn out from under a live client. **WHY IT CAME BACK — this was the SECOND kill** (first: pid 16242, trust-boundary phase 0). The `LaunchAgent` has **`RunAtLoad=true`** and **`KeepAlive={SuccessfulExit=false}`**, and its program is a `bash -c` wrapper whose FIRST act is **`lsof -ti:7860 | xargs kill -9`** — it force-kills whatever holds the port and takes it. **`launchctl print` showed `runs = 2`.** A manual `kill` could never have held; **killing the process was never the fix, unloading the job is.** **COMMANDS: `launchctl disable` THEN `launchctl bootout`** (both exit 0). **`bootout` alone would NOT have satisfied the dispatch** — it stops the job now, but `~/Library/LaunchAgents` is loaded at every login and the plist stays on disk by instruction, so the next login would undo it. **`disable` writes a persistent override into launchd's own store, keyed by label, independent of the file, surviving reboot.** `disable` first so `KeepAlive` had no window to relaunch between the two. Re-enabling is now a deliberate two-step (`enable` + `bootstrap`). **AFTER, all four:** nothing listening on 7860 ✓ · pid 72415 gone ✓ · `launchctl print` → *Could not find service* ✓ · `print-disabled` → **`"com.hip.voice.orch" => disabled`** ✓. **PLIST ON DISK, byte-unchanged:** `[REDACTED-USER-PATH]/Library/LaunchAgents/com.hip.voice.orch.plist` (1406 bytes, Aug 12 19:57). **⚠ FILED NOT FIXED: the plist carries THREE live secrets in cleartext** — `GROQ_API_KEY`, `NEO4J_PASSWORD`, `SERPAPI_KEY`; **values deliberately not reproduced and redacted from every captured output**. File is `-rw-r--r--` world-readable, and `launchctl print` exposes them to any process running as this user regardless of file mode. **Adjacent to FM 4** (credential rotation, STOPPED); **unloading a service does not rotate a credential.** **NEEDS BILL: whether these three join FM 4's rotation scope.** **ALSO RECORDED — Bill's three confirmations:** F5 is **POST-FREEZE**; **`TD-V-Pnnn` pointer rows are a standing convention** (cross-lane findability, not a duplicate ID, does not re-open item 10); **the freeze is pending VD-62** (gate the open read endpoints, retire `/ws/voice`, recertify). -- symlink LATEST_DISPATCH_FM10.md -- 2026-08-14 |

**a stored second copy would be a second source of truth about what a member has granted** and would disagree the first time an event was replayed, backfilled or corrected. GOVERNING REQ: `REQ_OFFER_MECHANISM__…__v20260806_1625.md` — **RULING 5**, **R20** control-plane isolation, **R21** no downstream interpretation, **R22** audit use is non-optimizing. **Nothing ruled MET; fixtures only.** Built `harness/control_plane_isolation.py` + `eval/test_control_plane_isolation.py` (**38 tests**). **TWO MECHANISMS, BECAUSE EITHER ALONE IS INSUFFICIENT:** a **write boundary** catches a runtime attempt but is blind to an import not yet called; a **structural import-closure ban** catches the attempt being *possible* but is blind to a runtime dictionary key. The write boundary alone would be behavioural, and a behavioural check only fires on the call someone actually makes — **the structural ban is what makes "a decline cannot reach the graph" a property of the code rather than a promise about it.** **THE WRITE BOUNDARY HAS NO SUCCESS PATH, DELIBERATELY:** `refuse_member_fact_write` always refuses and a test asserts its body contains no conditional — *a function that sometimes permitted an offer response into the graph would need a rule for when, and Ruling 5 does not have one.* **R20's four reads are NAMED BY THE CALLER** (`suppress_spent_offer`, `member_own_history`, `validate_grant_state`, `compliance_audit`); anything else is refused. The failure mode guarded is not malice — *"we needed the data" is how a compliance read becomes a personalization read without anyone deciding to make it one*, and naming which of the four makes the fifth use visible at the call site. **R22: CONVERSION METRICS BANNED BY EXISTENCE, NOT USE** — an `acceptance_rate` counter is a conversion metric whether or not anything reads it, because its existence makes optimizing on it a one-line change. **FOUR FAULT TWINS EXECUTED, ANTI-VACUITY FIRST** (every absence test passes trivially if the decline never happened — *Ruling 5 says a decline LIVES in the control plane, not that it vanishes*): (d) DECLINED, spent, transitions `['PRESENTED','DECLINED']`, re-presentation refused; (a) an attempted write **refused and recorded for every destination Ruling 5 names** — graph, embeddings, summaries, traits, trust, vulnerability, care-team projections, model context, inference inputs, operator optimization — with the refusal record carrying the SHAPE of the attempt and never the response, *because a refusal log that copied the response out of the control plane would be the leak it is refusing*; (b) **THE LOAD-BEARING TWIN — after a decline, the REAL assembled `local_system_prompt` contains no `situation_id`, `offer_instance_id`, `authority_delta_id`, "DECLINED" or capability name**, built via `TurnOrchestrator` exactly as layer 7's ctx-strip probe builds it, **checking the OUTPUT SURFACE rather than the code paths leading to it** — it would catch a decline arriving in model context by ANY route, including a well-meaning "the member declined this" hint, because it does not care how the text got there; the graph half asserted separately via `read_user_facts`; (c) a scratch `memory_engine.store` import injected into `spend_ledger` turned the scan **RED**, restored **byte-for-byte** (`git diff --stat` clean), green again — plus a **standing** form of the same twin that mutates nothing, scanning `memory_engine.store`/`fact_change`/`orchestrator` as entries so the check is proven red-capable on every run without an edit a crash could leave behind. **CLEAN ON THE FIRST RUN, and the reason is named rather than claimed as credit: HA-22's leaf-module split of `CANONICAL_ATTRIBUTES` already keeps `extraction_queue` out of this closure too — one fix, two requirements.** **A CATEGORY-COVERAGE TEST CAUGHT MY OWN PARAPHRASE:** it asserts every category Ruling 5 names maps to a real forbidden module, and failed until my descriptions matched the ruling's words ("model context", not "model-context") — it checks Bill's wording, not mine. Anti-vacuity: zero modules scanned, a non-expanding closure, and a forbidden destination that does not exist are all refusals (a rename would otherwise disarm the guard silently forever), plus the metric-fragment list must actually match probes. **A FALSE CLAIM IN A DRAFT OF THIS DISPATCH WAS CAUGHT BEFORE PUBLISHING:** a draft §5 said the new module was declared to HA-22's purity scan. **It was not, and it should not be** — `control_plane_isolation.py` is a checker, holds no offer state and renders nothing, so it belongs in neither `OFFER_PATH_ENTRY_MODULES` nor `CONTROL_PLANE_MODULES`; its `FORBIDDEN_DESTINATIONS` are name STRINGS, not imports, so a self-scan would prove nothing. Corrected in the doc rather than made true by adding a line of code. **RIDER — CLAIMS LEDGER v3:** C-14 added at Bill's wording verbatim (PARTIAL), three dropped citations restored, v2 superseded and retained unaltered, LATEST repointed, MANIFEST + INDEX updated; **the rider's "six" names THREE CELLS and the discrepancy is reported, not resolved silently.** RUNS: batteries **934 passed / 0 failed** (896→934), L7 27/27, L7V2 27/28, AUDIT 9/9, `KEY-HYGIENE-ZERO-ORPHAN` PASS, **RATCHET PASS at exit 0**, memory harness **13/17 inside the pin**. **CLAIM IMPACT: C-06, C-14** — C-06 reinforced (a spent situation refuses re-presentation *after a terminal transition*, where the guarantee is load-bearing), C-14 recorded as newly existing; **HA-26 produced no new evidence for C-14 — its evidence is HA-25's battery.** **FLAGGED: RULING 5 ITSELF HAS NO LEDGER CLAIM** — the isolation built here is a substantial privacy guarantee with nothing covering it, the same gap HA-25 flagged for R16 before C-14 closed it; **cap 15, 14 used.** **DOES NOT CLAIM:** the structural ban is static (a computed dynamic import would evade it — same disclosed limit as HA-22); the prompt twin proves the offer control plane puts nothing into prompt assembly, not that every turn shape is clean; nothing enabled, no classifier, HA-25's gap stands.
